# Planner brief: backend test-suite speed-up packet (2026-10-14)

Role: Planner (Codex). Branch `plan/test-suite-speed` (worktree `C:\Users\assef\Bineyame\AssetOpsV1-gap-planning`, from main). Owner instruction (verbatim, `.agent/owner-process-speed-2026-10-14.md`): "do item 1 after S4-02d and add the rest based on observation and merit". This is a tooling task run right after S4-02d, ahead of normal next-work ranking (like PRE-S3-03: outside competitive W1 scoring; do not give it a W1 core score).

Problem observed today: the full backend suite (~2100 tests, Postgres-backed) takes 11-16 min alone and 23-35 min when another agent uses Postgres; implementers and reviewers each run it per phase; concurrent runs hit "too many clients".

Write ONE implementation-ready packet `tasks/T-test-speed-backend-suite.md` (rename if the repo convention suggests better; tell me):
- Goal: cut full-suite wall time substantially without dropping or weakening any test; measured before/after on this Windows dev machine.
- Investigate and specify from the ACTUAL code (code-reality check is mandatory: name only fixtures, conftest files, scripts and env vars that exist, with file:line): how tests get their Postgres database today (find the harness that requires `_test` DB names, per-test or per-module migration, engine/connection handling, `gc.collect()` workarounds), how `tools/check-all.ps1` and agent briefs run the suite.
- Options to evaluate and choose among, with justification: pytest-xdist parallel workers with one DB per worker; a migrated template DB cloned via `CREATE DATABASE ... TEMPLATE` instead of re-running migrations; connection-pool limits per worker; test selection markers (fast/unit vs Postgres) so phases run focused subsets; a documented "focused set" command per area. Keep determinism; handle tests that start subprocesses, advisory locks, or rely on a single shared DB.
- Process outputs: a short testing guide (where it belongs in the repo) stating: full suite once per slice at the end; per-phase focused/affected suites; reviewers rerun the full suite only when a finding needs it; at most one full suite at a time on this machine unless the new isolation makes concurrency safe.
- Phases (S/M sizing; each fits one Claude implementer context) with focused proof: identical pass count before/after, no skipped tests added, timing table, two concurrent full runs without "too many clients" if claimed.
- USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED false (no product UI); independent review required.
- Registry: decide whether it needs a registry node at all (tooling outside W1 scoring); if so mirror how PRE-S3-03 or other outside-ranking work is recorded and keep `check_v69_dependency_registry.cjs` passing. Add a `tasks/README.md` row in the appropriate lane.

| --- | --- | --- |
| S1 | Existing Site shell/Foundation presentation and explicit Kobo fixture | YAML/JSON Site writes for the supported Foundation path become Postgres revisions; old files remain read-only comparison inputs. |

| S3-S5 | Existing dispatch, Finding and Site presentation where claim semantics still fit | Hard-coded device keys, JSON assessment authority and independent view recalculation give way to typed accepted-evidence inputs, revisioned claims and derived read models. |
| S6-S7 | Existing Lab control and Site shell where they fit | Persist Action/verification/health claims from public evidence and work records; no Lab truth or static mock value becomes product history. |

This is a cutover map, not permission to delete historical fixtures or completed task evidence. Do not call the current in-process publisher or JSON ingestion the production gateway/ledger.

param(
    # Override the interpreter used for Python guards (default: repository .venv).
    [string]$Python = "",
    [string]$Node = "node"
)

# Normal local/CI check command. Runs every repository guard, reports each one,
# and exits nonzero when any guard fails or a required tool is missing.
#
#   powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tools\check-all.ps1
#
# A guard that cannot run is a failure, never a skip. Do not weaken a guard to
# make this pass.

$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"
$repoRoot = Split-Path -Parent $PSScriptRoot
Push-Location $repoRoot

try {
    if (-not $Python) { $Python = Join-Path $repoRoot ".venv\Scripts\python.exe" }
    $powershell = (Get-Process -Id $PID).Path

    $results = New-Object System.Collections.Generic.List[object]

    function Resolve-Tool([string]$tool) {
        if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $tool -PathType Leaf) { return $tool }
        $cmd = Get-Command $tool -CommandType Application -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Select-Object -First 1
        if ($cmd) { return $cmd.Source }
        return $null
    }

    function Invoke-Guard([string]$name, [string]$tool, [string[]]$arguments) {
        $resolved = Resolve-Tool $tool
        if (-not $resolved) {
            $results.Add([pscustomobject]@{ Guard = $name; Status = "FAIL"; Detail = "missing tool: $tool" }) | Out-Null
            return
        }
        $previous = $ErrorActionPreference
        $ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"
        $output = & $resolved @arguments 2>&1 | ForEach-Object { "$_" }
        $code = $LASTEXITCODE
        $ErrorActionPreference = $previous
        $last = @($output | Where-Object { $_.Trim() }) | Select-Object -Last 1
        if ($code -eq 0) {
            $results.Add([pscustomobject]@{ Guard = $name; Status = "PASS"; Detail = $last }) | Out-Null
        } else {
            $results.Add([pscustomobject]@{ Guard = $name; Status = "FAIL"; Detail = "exit $code" ; Output = $output }) | Out-Null
        }
    }

    $navigationScript = @'
$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"
. (Join-Path $PWD "tools/checks/_common.ps1")
. (Join-Path $PWD "tools/checks/navigation-truthfulness.ps1")
$failures = @(Invoke-NavigationTruthfulnessCheck)
if ($failures.Count -gt 0) { $failures | ForEach-Object { Write-Output "- $_" }; exit 1 }
Write-Output "Navigation truthfulness check passed."
'@

    Invoke-Guard "Architecture seams"            $powershell @("-NoProfile", "-ExecutionPolicy", "Bypass", "-File", "tools/check-architecture.ps1")
    Invoke-Guard "Navigation truthfulness"       $powershell @("-NoProfile", "-ExecutionPolicy", "Bypass", "-EncodedCommand",
        [Convert]::ToBase64String([Text.Encoding]::Unicode.GetBytes($navigationScript)))
    Invoke-Guard "Agent workflow"                $powershell @("-NoProfile", "-ExecutionPolicy", "Bypass", "-File", "tools/check-agent-workflow.ps1")
    Invoke-Guard "Dependency registry"           $Node @("frontend/docs/architecture/check_v69_dependency_registry.cjs")
    Invoke-Guard "Delivery checker fixtures"     $Node @("--test", "frontend/docs/architecture/test_delivery_next_work.cjs")
    Invoke-Guard "Mock-gap catalogue fixtures"   $Node @("--test", "frontend/docs/architecture/test_mock_gaps.cjs")
    Invoke-Guard "Missing-claim orphan scan"     $Node @("tools/checks/missing-claim-scan.mjs")
    Invoke-Guard "Missing-claim scan fixtures"   $Node @("--test", "tools/checks/test_missing_claim_scan.mjs")
    Invoke-Guard "Screen ledger"                 $Python @("frontend/docs/architecture/check_v69_ledger.py")

    $failed = @($results | Where-Object { $_.Status -ne "PASS" })
    foreach ($result in $results) {
        Write-Host ("[{0}] {1}: {2}" -f $result.Status, $result.Guard, $result.Detail)
        if ($result.Status -ne "PASS" -and $result.Output) {
            $result.Output | Select-Object -Last 15 | ForEach-Object { Write-Host "    $_" }
        }
    }
    if ($failed.Count -gt 0) {
        Write-Host "Check failed: $($failed.Count) of $($results.Count) guards."
        exit 1
    }
    Write-Host "All $($results.Count) guards passed."
    exit 0
}
finally {
    Pop-Location
}

 succeeded in 2095ms:
param(
    # Override the interpreter used for Python guards (default: repository .venv).
    [string]$Python = "",
    [string]$Node = "node"
)

# Normal local/CI check command. Runs every repository guard, reports each one,
# and exits nonzero when any guard fails or a required tool is missing.
#
#   powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File tools\check-all.ps1
#
# A guard that cannot run is a failure, never a skip. Do not weaken a guard to
# make this pass.

$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"
$repoRoot = Split-Path -Parent $PSScriptRoot
Push-Location $repoRoot

try {
    if (-not $Python) { $Python = Join-Path $repoRoot ".venv\Scripts\python.exe" }
    $powershell = (Get-Process -Id $PID).Path

    $results = New-Object System.Collections.Generic.List[object]

    function Resolve-Tool([string]$tool) {
        if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $tool -PathType Leaf) { return $tool }
        $cmd = Get-Command $tool -CommandType Application -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue | Select-Object -First 1
        if ($cmd) { return $cmd.Source }
        return $null
    }

    function Invoke-Guard([string]$name, [string]$tool, [string[]]$arguments) {
        $resolved = Resolve-Tool $tool
        if (-not $resolved) {
            $results.Add([pscustomobject]@{ Guard = $name; Status = "FAIL"; Detail = "missing tool: $tool" }) | Out-Null
            return
        }
        $previous = $ErrorActionPreference
        $ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"
        $output = & $resolved @arguments 2>&1 | ForEach-Object { "$_" }
        $code = $LASTEXITCODE
        $ErrorActionPreference = $previous
        $last = @($output | Where-Object { $_.Trim() }) | Select-Object -Last 1
        if ($code -eq 0) {
            $results.Add([pscustomobject]@{ Guard = $name; Status = "PASS"; Detail = $last }) | Out-Null
        } else {
            $results.Add([pscustomobject]@{ Guard = $name; Status = "FAIL"; Detail = "exit $code" ; Output = $output }) | Out-Null
        }
    }

    $navigationScript = @'
$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"
. (Join-Path $PWD "tools/checks/_common.ps1")
. (Join-Path $PWD "tools/checks/navigation-truthfulness.ps1")
$failures = @(Invoke-NavigationTruthfulnessCheck)
if ($failures.Count -gt 0) { $failures | ForEach-Object { Write-Output "- $_" }; exit 1 }
Write-Output "Navigation truthfulness check passed."
'@

    Invoke-Guard "Architecture seams"            $powershell @("-NoProfile", "-ExecutionPolicy", "Bypass", "-File", "tools/check-architecture.ps1")
    Invoke-Guard "Navigation truthfulness"       $powershell @("-NoProfile", "-ExecutionPolicy", "Bypass", "-EncodedCommand",
        [Convert]::ToBase64String([Text.Encoding]::Unicode.GetBytes($navigationScript)))
    Invoke-Guard "Agent workflow"                $powershell @("-NoProfile", "-ExecutionPolicy", "Bypass", "-File", "tools/check-agent-workflow.ps1")
    Invoke-Guard "Dependency registry"           $Node @("frontend/docs/architecture/check_v69_dependency_registry.cjs")
    Invoke-Guard "Delivery checker fixtures"     $Node @("--test", "frontend/docs/architecture/test_delivery_next_work.cjs")
    Invoke-Guard "Mock-gap catalogue fixtures"   $Node @("--test", "frontend/docs/architecture/test_mock_gaps.cjs")
    Invoke-Guard "Missing-claim orphan scan"     $Node @("tools/checks/missing-claim-scan.mjs")
    Invoke-Guard "Missing-claim scan fixtures"   $Node @("--test", "tools/checks/test_missing_claim_scan.mjs")
    Invoke-Guard "Screen ledger"                 $Python @("frontend/docs/architecture/check_v69_ledger.py")

    $failed = @($results | Where-Object { $_.Status -ne "PASS" })
    foreach ($result in $results) {
        Write-Host ("[{0}] {1}: {2}" -f $result.Status, $result.Guard, $result.Detail)
        if ($result.Status -ne "PASS" -and $result.Output) {
            $result.Output | Select-Object -Last 15 | ForEach-Object { Write-Host "    $_" }
        }
    }
    if ($failed.Count -gt 0) {
        Write-Host "Check failed: $($failed.Count) of $($results.Count) guards."
        exit 1
    }
    Write-Host "All $($results.Count) guards passed."
    exit 0
}
finally {
    Pop-Location
}

Include a mixed S1/S2 panel fixture with one evidenced delivered portion, one open positive portion and V-01 pending: delivered S1/S2 nodes contribute no reach or distance, the positive portion stays open, V-01 can receive visual reach, and no panel is counted twice. All invalid cases fail closed with an actionable reason. Existing registry, ledger and navigation checks continue to pass.

## Normal check path

Wire registry, ledger, navigation and workflow guards into the normal local/CI check command documented for this repo. A clean run reports each guard; a missing tool or nonzero guard exits nonzero. The coordinator verifies this prerequisite before activating ranking or S3-01. Owner W1 input is recorded; the report remains non-authoritative until mapped paths and scoring are independently reviewed.
# Product database (S1-01)

The persisted Foundation lives in PostgreSQL 16, run locally from the pinned
service in `docker-compose.yml` (`postgres:16.4-alpine`). Alembic migrations in
`backend/migrations` own every table; the application never creates schema.

`DATABASE_URL` is required on the product path. Nothing in the backend
defaults it: without it the product Sites index and Foundation routes answer
`503` (fail closed) rather than treating every Site as unimported.

## Local development values

The Compose file documents one **local development** default password
(`assetops-local-dev`, override with `ASSETOPS_PG_PASSWORD`) and publishes the
service on `127.0.0.1:55432` (override with `ASSETOPS_PG_PORT`). It is not a
credential for any shared environment.

```sh
# from the repository root
docker compose up -d postgres

# developer database
export DATABASE_URL=postgresql+psycopg://assetops:assetops-local-dev@127.0.0.1:55432/assetops
# PowerShell: $env:DATABASE_URL = "postgresql+psycopg://assetops:assetops-local-dev@127.0.0.1:55432/assetops"

# migrate (from backend/)
cd backend && ../.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m alembic upgrade head && cd ..

# one-shot import of the Kobo Site document (from the repository root)
.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m assetops_backend.sites.import_kobo \
  --source var/sites/kobo-001.yaml --tenant demo \
  --report .agent/S1-01-import-comparison.md

# S1-02: publish the first signal binding set (one atomic Foundation-plus-binding
# change; a rerun writes nothing). Decision D-2026-10-08-s1-bindings-roles-and-snapshot.
.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m assetops_backend.sites.convert_kobo_bindings   --fixture backend/conversions/kobo-001-bindings.json --tenant demo

# serve
.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m uvicorn assetops_backend.main:app --port 8000
```

The first migration enables `btree_gist`. A database role that cannot create
the extension fails the migration with a message naming the fix; there is no
fallback.

Migration 0003 (pinned snapshots) refuses to run over binding sets published
under 0002 alone, because their snapshot hash cannot be backfilled honestly.
That state exists only on a scratch database used during S1-02 development.
Recreate that database and rerun the conversion.

Migration 0004 stores each pinned snapshot's canonical bytes
(`snapshot_canonical`, NOT NULL, CHECKed against `snapshot_sha256`); the
download serves those bytes instead of rebuilding them from registration rows.
Over a database that already holds binding sets (for example a converted
developer database) it backfills each row with the v1 serializer, and writes the
bytes only when they reproduce the pinned hash. If a registration identity field
(Site code, gateway external ID or mode, source external ID) changed after
publication, the rebuilt bytes miss the hash and the migration refuses, naming
the rows; it never recomputes a published hash.

## Tests

The Compose service also creates `assetops_test`, a disposable database used
only by the Postgres tests. They drop its schema and run every migration from
zero; they never touch the developer database.

```sh
export ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL=postgresql+psycopg://assetops:assetops-local-dev@127.0.0.1:55432/assetops_test
.venv/Scripts/python.exe -m pytest backend/tests/test_s1_foundation_postgres.py backend/tests/test_s1_bindings_postgres.py
```

Without `ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL` those tests are reported as skipped, not
passed. They refuse to run against a database whose name does not end in
`_test`.

## Consequence recompute runner (S4-02c)

Financial consequence work (migration 0011/0012 triggers) is drained by a one-shot, bounded runner for one
explicitly named tenant and Site. It is not an all-Site iterator and serves only the configured development
tenant (`demo`); any other tenant is refused with exit code 2.

```sh
# from backend/, with DATABASE_URL set
python -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences run --tenant demo --site KOBO-001 --limit 100
python -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences pending --tenant demo --site KOBO-001
```

- **Schedule:** an external timer runs it every minute, one task per Site. Windows Task Scheduler:
  `schtasks /Create /TN "AssetOps consequence runner KOBO-001" /SC MINUTE /MO 1 /TR "<venv>\Scripts\python.exe -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences run --tenant demo --site KOBO-001 --limit 100"`
  with "Do not start a new instance" and a start-in directory of `backend/`. systemd: a `Type=oneshot`
  `assetops-consequence-runner@.service` (`ExecStart=<venv>/bin/python -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences run --tenant demo --site %i --limit 100`,
  `EnvironmentFile` holding `DATABASE_URL`) and a matching `.timer` with `OnCalendar=*-*-* *:*:00`.
- **Owner:** whoever operates the deployment's backend host.
- **Overlap:** the run takes a session advisory lock `financial_consequence_runner:<site>` on a dedicated
  connection; a second run for the same Site reports `"skipped": true` and exits 0.
- **Recovery:** nothing manual. A failed item records a typed FAILED attempt and stays pending; it is retried
  1, 2, 4, 8, 16 minutes after its last failure, then every 16 minutes, with no cap. A killed run's lock is
  released with its connection; the next tick resumes, and an append-then-crash replays the same consequence ID.
- **Hung runner (alarm):** the run is not bounded by a timeout. A run that hangs (rather than dies) keeps its
  connection and its Site claim, so every later tick prints `"skipped": true` and still exits 0, and "Do not
  start a new instance" hides it further. Repeated `skipped` across consecutive ticks for one Site is the alarm
  signal: find the holding session (`pg_locks` advisory lock on `financial_consequence_runner:<site>`, or the
  stuck process) and end it; its lock is released with its connection and the next tick resumes.
- **Permanent failures (retry volume):** a deterministic failure (for example `EVIDENCE_NOT_ACCEPTED`,
  `EVIDENCE_LOCATOR_NOT_IN_SITE` or a `RECOMPUTE_FAILED` code defect) fails on every retry and appends about 90
  FAILED attempts per day per row, indefinitely; there is no terminal state or alert. Watch `pending` for rows
  whose attempt count keeps growing and repair the cause. Past roughly 1600 such rows per Site at `--limit 100`
  they alone would fill each run's capacity (FIFO-by-eligibility still bounds new work's delay, but the backlog
  grows).
- **Output:** one JSON line per processed item, then a summary line with the Site scope and the `selected`,

   1: """Test configuration shared by the backend suite.
   2: 
   3: Its only job is to keep pytest's temporary directories somewhere the current
   4: process can actually write.
   5: 
   6: Why. `tmp_path` and `tmp_path_factory` default to a base under the user's temp
   7: directory. That is fine on a developer machine and fails in a sandboxed one: a
   8: Reviewer agent whose write access stops at the workspace gets
   9: `PermissionError: [WinError 5] Access is denied` during fixture setup, on every
  10: test that asks for a temp path. Three consecutive reviews of this repository
  11: could not run the backend suite for that reason, so their verification of the
  12: Implementer's numbers was missing. This file fixes the backend half only; the
  13: frontend suite cannot run in that sandbox at all, for an unrelated reason
  14: recorded in the Reviewer brief.
  15: 
  16: The default is left alone whenever it works, which matters more than it looks.
  17: An earlier version of this file always redirected the base into the
  18: repository, and that broke the suite in a new way: the directory ends up owned
  19: by whichever process created it first, and the other one is then denied. The
  20: developer run would lock out the sandboxed run, the sandboxed run would lock
  21: out the developer, and each would look like a fresh permissions bug. So the
  22: redirect now happens only when the default is unusable, which means the
  23: developer machine never creates the directory the sandbox needs to own.
  24: 
  25: The workspace path is derived from this file's location rather than from the
  26: working directory, because the suite is run both from the repository root
  27: (`pytest backend/tests`) and from `backend/`, and a relative path would mean
  28: two different places. If even that is not writable - a leftover owned by
  29: another identity - a process-unique sibling is used instead, because failing to
  30: find a temp directory should never be the reason a suite cannot run.
  31: 
  32: An explicit `--basetemp` on the command line always wins.

  47: def _is_writable(directory: Path) -> bool:
  48:     """Whether this process can actually create a file in `directory`."""
  49:     try:
  50:         directory.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  51:         probe = directory / f".write-probe-{os.getpid()}"
  52:         probe.touch()
  53:         probe.unlink()
  54:     except OSError:
  55:         return False
  56:     return True

  60:     """The directory pytest will actually use, not the temp root above it.
  61: 
  62:     pytest does not write into the temp root; it writes into a `pytest-of-<user>`
  63:     directory inside it. That distinction is the whole problem: the temp root is
  64:     writable by everyone, while `pytest-of-<user>` belongs to whichever process
  65:     created it first and denies the other. Probing the root reports success and
  66:     then pytest fails one level down, which is exactly how an earlier version of
  67:     this file let the sandboxed suite regress from 304 passing to 89 errors.
  68:     """
  69:     user = os.environ.get("USER") or os.environ.get("USERNAME") or "unknown"

# Site-first task queue

**Current direction (2026-10-07):** [current system architecture](../frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md), [current technical design](../frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md), and [Site-first execution plan](../frontend/docs/architecture/site-first-execution-plan.md). These are current implementation authority under project routing; their proposal headers do not record owner sign-off. The Architect decision `D-2026-10-07-s1-foundation-import-and-cutover` and S1-01 addendum control the first slice. This is the only executable queue. The [superseded T0xx queue](archive/pre-site-first-2026-10-07/README.md) and its open packets are historical reference with original status intact. Completed tasks and decisions remain historical built-state evidence.

One mini-grid Site and one assessed window form the first end-to-end path. S1-01/02/03 and S2-01a/b/c/S2-02 are reviewed and merged through `82ba6cd`; the owner passed the S2 Evidence checkpoint and adopted V-01 on 2026-10-09. The owner confirmed W1's revised 27-screen target, core panels, demo values and K=10 on 2026-10-11 (`.agent/owner-W1-confirmation-2026-10-11.md`). S3â€“S7 remain outlines. Startup guards and S3 dispatch contract/fixtures remain activation gates. S2-02r is a follow-up before unattended/multi-operator rotation or a real pilot, not a dispatch prerequisite.

For implementation, read the active packet and the contract sections it names. The registry, ledger and follow-on lanes below guide Architect/Planner review; they do not expand an active packet's scope.

**Pre-S3 planning gates:** `A-claim-work-routes`, `A-actor-identity` and bounded `A-evidence-readiness-health` are recorded in the pre-S3 decisions. `A-finding-index`, `A-money-comparability` and broad shared projections remain open. S4-01 and S4-02 consume the route and actor decisions; S6-01 reuses both accepted contracts. These decisions do not add implementation prerequisites to dispatch. At each S2â€“S7 outline refinement, compare the packet with the registry's `queue_baseline_nodes`; a contract or fixture no longer owned by the queue leaves that baseline before marginal estimates are used.

**Simulation and edge decision:** `D-2026-10-07-s2-edge-split-and-demo-source` sets S2-01a/b/c and a simulated-only S2 demo. S2-02 owns authenticated upload and central diagnostics; the first real pilot chooses a real capture or labelled vendor-format fixture for physical protocol proof. `A-edge-batch-signature` defers batch signatures/key rotation; TLS gateway credentials, canonical digest and exact-byte retry remain required. S3-01 owns dispatch scenarios, S6-02 owns two-run verification scenarios, and causal battery-stress production remains outside S7. At each outline refinement, the Planner checks `queue_baseline_nodes` against the refined packet; technical `requires` remain separate from queue order.

The recorded `A-lab-registered-basis`, `A-edge-diagnostics` and `A-demo-scenarios` contracts are consumed by S2-01a/c and later packets. S2-01c owns `I-edge-fault-scenarios`; S3-01 owns `I-sim-dispatch-cases`; S6-02 owns `I-sim-verification-cases`. `A-real-peer-source` records simulated-only demo input; the first real pilot records its physical peer before refining an adapter task.

+# T-test-speed — Backend test-suite turnaround
+
+Status: implementation-ready; activate immediately after reviewed/merged S4-02d, before ordinary next-work ranking, per `.agent/owner-process-speed-2026-10-14.md:6`. Tooling work, outside competitive W1 scoring. Size: M, split into three S phases. Branch: `task/T-test-speed-backend-suite` (from the then-current main). USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED: false. Independent review: required.
+
+## Outcome and limits
+
+Substantially reduce wall time for the complete, real-Postgres backend suite on this Windows development machine without dropping, skipping, weakening, or silently selecting tests. Record a same-revision, same-machine baseline and final timing, exact collected/passed/skipped/failed counts, database settings, worker count and command. A full run with the test DB unset is invalid proof. Preserve migration upgrade/downgrade, process, concurrency and advisory-lock coverage. No product UI or W1 panel claim.
+
+## Current code, checked 2026-10-14
+
+| Finding | Evidence |
+| --- | --- |
+| `ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL` is the real-Postgres switch; absent DB causes skips, and destructive fixtures require a `*_test` database. | `backend/tests/test_s1_foundation_postgres.py:21-24,66-81`; `backend/README-db.md:76-90` |
+| `backend/tests/conftest.py` only chooses a writable pytest temp base; it does not provision or isolate Postgres. Preserve explicit `--basetemp` and its fallback behavior. | `backend/tests/conftest.py:1-4,73-91` |
+| The module-scoped `engine` fixture drops/recreates `public`, drops `btree_gist`, runs Alembic to head, and disposes its engine. Function-scoped `store` truncates rows but returns a store with its own engine. | `backend/tests/test_s1_foundation_postgres.py:66-90`; `backend/tests/test_s1_bindings_postgres.py:64-82` |
+| S4 files import the S1 binding fixture rather than a common DB fixture; each importing module executes its own module fixture. A separate S3 demo test also resets and migrates. Migration-history tests deliberately downgrade/upgrade in place. | `backend/tests/test_s4_assumptions_postgres.py:27-29`; `backend/tests/test_s4_consequence_store_postgres.py:36-38,387-397`; `backend/tests/test_s3_demo_configuration.py:138-149`; `backend/tests/test_s4_evidence_consequence_work_postgres.py:464-544` |
+| Short-lived stores can retain connections until garbage collection; three S4 modules use per-test `gc.collect()` as a slot workaround. A drain test starts a child Python process against `URL`, and advisory locks are asserted. | `backend/tests/test_s4_consequence_store_postgres.py:26-33`; `backend/tests/test_s4_consequence_recompute_postgres.py:23-30`; `backend/tests/test_s4_power_demo_case_postgres.py:27-34`; `backend/tests/test_s4_evidence_drain_postgres.py:398-431` |
+| The normal `tools/check-all.ps1` command is nine repository guards, not pytest. Agent handoffs separately report full backend pytest plus guards, sometimes per phase. | `tools/check-all.ps1:8-13,70-80`; `.agent/S3-01-phase-1-handoff.md:35`; `.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-handoff.md:115` |
+| pytest currently has no xdist dependency or marker definitions. | `backend/pyproject.toml:23-39` |
+
+## Chosen approach and rejected shortcuts
+
+1. **First remove repeated head migrations from ordinary Postgres setup.** Build one clean, migrated `*_test` template for each run, then clone to isolated worker DBs with `CREATE DATABASE ... TEMPLATE` from an admin connection outside the template. Clones must use names ending `_test`; source template must have no open sessions, and all generated DBs need explicit owner/run identity and cleanup in `finally`. Assert migration head on each clone. Keep the existing destructive upgrade/downgrade tests on dedicated clones that no ordinary test shares. If cloning is unavailable under the local role, report it and keep the tested serial migration path; never fall back to a shared live DB while claiming isolation. Rework fixture ownership so ordinary module setup truncates and never drops another module's schema. Preserve any tests whose own reset/migration is their assertion.
+2. **Then use bounded pytest-xdist workers, one DB per worker.** Add xdist to backend dev dependencies. Use worker identity only for DB routing; child processes inherit the worker URL. Begin with `-n 2`, measure `-n 3` only if connection counts remain safe, and pick the fastest stable count. Do not use `--dist load` if order-sensitive modules share state; use module grouping or explicit serial group for migration/process/lock tests. One worker DB is not sufficient if tests in that worker race a child process or a module rebuilds schema: identify and serialize or isolate those cases before enabling the default command. No `-n auto` on this machine.
+3. **Bound connections rather than assuming worker DB isolation bounds sessions.** Give test-created SQLAlchemy engines small pools (initial candidate `pool_size=1, max_overflow=1`) and dispose stores/clients explicitly where ownership permits. Account for migration `NullPool`, child processes and in-test concurrency when sizing; collect actual peak `pg_stat_activity` and retain legitimate concurrency assertions. `gc.collect()` removal is optional only after proof that engine lifecycle is deterministic. Do not alter production pool defaults simply to improve tests.
+4. **Focused suites are a workflow speed gain, not a replacement full run.** Add documented area commands and, only if useful after collection audit, explicit `unit`/`postgres` markers for focused selection. Missing DB must remain visible as skipped and disqualify full proof; markers must never deselect tests from the full command. File-path focus can ship before marker changes. Avoid a broad auto-marker that mislabels mixed files such as `test_s3_demo_configuration.py:126-149`.
+
+## Implementation phases and proof
+
+| Phase | Build (one implementer context) | Focused proof / exit |
+| --- | --- | --- |
+| 1 (S) baseline and guide | Record serial full-suite baseline on a private `*_test` DB with `--durations=30`, collection count, skip reasons, peak sessions and wall time; inventory module migration calls, engine owners, child-process and lock tests. Add a short guide at `backend/TESTING.md` linked from `backend/README-db.md`; include exact area-focused commands. | Guide commands run; no test edits or changed pass/skip counts. Baseline has zero DB-related skips. Record environment and timing in review packet. |
+| 2 (S) template and lifecycle | Implement run-owned migrated template/clone harness; move common setup out of S1 test modules; preserve destructive migration tests on dedicated clone(s); explicit disposal and bounded test pools. Keep a serial full command working. | Focused S1/S2/S3/S4 Postgres files, migration downgrade/upgrade cases, child-process/advisory-lock cases and a serial full suite. Equal collected/pass/skip totals to phase-1 baseline on same revision (new harness tests count separately); zero DB skips; template cleanup after failure. Timing table serial before/after. |
+| 3 (S) bounded parallel | Add xdist command with proven per-worker DB routing, deterministic module/serial grouping and safe connection budget. Make the guide's full command the normal backend command. Run focused area sets in each phase, then one full suite at slice closeout. | Two repeat full runs: identical collected/pass/skip totals to serial (account explicitly for new harness tests), zero DB skips, no flaky order dependency; record serial/template/2-worker/optional 3-worker wall times and peak sessions. If claiming concurrent full runs safe, run two simultaneous full invocations on distinct run-owned DB sets and prove both pass without `too many clients`; otherwise document one-at-a-time policy. |
+
+The independent reviewer inspects provisioning, cleanup, isolation, pass-count and timing artifacts, reruns focused high-risk files, and reruns full only when a finding needs it. A failure to establish deterministic parallel execution leaves serial-with-template as the documented full command; do not assert a parallel speedup. Timing target: substantial improvement against the measured phase-1 baseline; if none, report the measured cause and revise the mechanism before closeout.
+
+## Testing guide contract
+
+`backend/TESTING.md` shall say: one real-Postgres full backend suite at the end of each slice; per-phase focused/affected test files (include adjacent integration tests and host/gateway suites when interfaces cross); reviewers rerun full only for a finding that needs it, otherwise cite the implementer's same-revision full evidence and run relevant focused tests. At most one full suite on this machine at a time until two concurrent isolated full runs prove safe. Include PowerShell examples for S1 Foundation/bindings, S2 ingestion, S3 dispatch, S4 Finding/money and the complete suite, with explicit DB environment setup and zero-skips check. `tools/check-all.ps1` remains the separate required guard command.
+
+## Registry and closeout
+
+No dependency-registry node: this is process/tooling work with no technical path to an approved W1 core panel. PRE-S3-03 is likewise a queue/guard packet without a registry task node (`tasks/PRE-S3-03-delivery-checker-report.md`; `frontend/docs/architecture/v69-dependency-registry-pilot.json:55-60`), while PRE-S3-04 has a product projection node. Queue row below records scheduling without inventing score or changing `queue_baseline_nodes`. Run `node frontend/docs/architecture/check_v69_dependency_registry.cjs` on planning and implementation diffs. Close only after independent review accepts unchanged coverage, deterministic result and measured speed on this Windows host.

+| The normal `tools/check-all.ps1` command is nine repository guards, not pytest. Agent handoffs separately report full backend pytest plus guards, sometimes per phase. | `tools/check-all.ps1:8-13,70-80`; `.agent/S3-01-phase-1-handoff.md:35`; `.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-handoff.md:115` |
+| pytest currently has no xdist dependency or marker definitions. | `backend/pyproject.toml:23-39` |
+
+## Chosen approach and rejected shortcuts
+
+1. **First remove repeated head migrations from ordinary Postgres setup.** Build one clean, migrated `*_test` template for each run, then clone to isolated worker DBs with `CREATE DATABASE ... TEMPLATE` from an admin connection outside the template. Clones must use names ending `_test`; source template must have no open sessions, and all generated DBs need explicit owner/run identity and cleanup in `finally`. Assert migration head on each clone. Keep the existing destructive upgrade/downgrade tests on dedicated clones that no ordinary test shares. If cloning is unavailable under the local role, report it and keep the tested serial migration path; never fall back to a shared live DB while claiming isolation. Rework fixture ownership so ordinary module setup truncates and never drops another module's schema. Preserve any tests whose own reset/migration is their assertion.
+2. **Then use bounded pytest-xdist workers, one DB per worker.** Add xdist to backend dev dependencies. Use worker identity only for DB routing; child processes inherit the worker URL. Begin with `-n 2`, measure `-n 3` only if connection counts remain safe, and pick the fastest stable count. Do not use `--dist load` if order-sensitive modules share state; use module grouping or explicit serial group for migration/process/lock tests. One worker DB is not sufficient if tests in that worker race a child process or a module rebuilds schema: identify and serialize or isolate those cases before enabling the default command. No `-n auto` on this machine.
+3. **Bound connections rather than assuming worker DB isolation bounds sessions.** Give test-created SQLAlchemy engines small pools (initial candidate `pool_size=1, max_overflow=1`) and dispose stores/clients explicitly where ownership permits. Account for migration `NullPool`, child processes and in-test concurrency when sizing; collect actual peak `pg_stat_activity` and retain legitimate concurrency assertions. `gc.collect()` removal is optional only after proof that engine lifecycle is deterministic. Do not alter production pool defaults simply to improve tests.
+4. **Focused suites are a workflow speed gain, not a replacement full run.** Add documented area commands and, only if useful after collection audit, explicit `unit`/`postgres` markers for focused selection. Missing DB must remain visible as skipped and disqualify full proof; markers must never deselect tests from the full command. File-path focus can ship before marker changes. Avoid a broad auto-marker that mislabels mixed files such as `test_s3_demo_configuration.py:126-149`.
+
+## Implementation phases and proof
+
+| Phase | Build (one implementer context) | Focused proof / exit |
+| --- | --- | --- |
+| 1 (S) baseline and guide | Record serial full-suite baseline on a private `*_test` DB with `--durations=30`, collection count, skip reasons, peak sessions and wall time; inventory module migration calls, engine owners, child-process and lock tests. Add a short guide at `backend/TESTING.md` linked from `backend/README-db.md`; include exact area-focused commands. | Guide commands run; no test edits or changed pass/skip counts. Baseline has zero DB-related skips. Record environment and timing in review packet. |
+| 2 (S) template and lifecycle | Implement run-owned migrated template/clone harness; move common setup out of S1 test modules; preserve destructive migration tests on dedicated clone(s); explicit disposal and bounded test pools. Keep a serial full command working. | Focused S1/S2/S3/S4 Postgres files, migration downgrade/upgrade cases, child-process/advisory-lock cases and a serial full suite. Equal collected/pass/skip totals to phase-1 baseline on same revision (new harness tests count separately); zero DB skips; template cleanup after failure. Timing table serial before/after. |
+| 3 (S) bounded parallel | Add xdist command with proven per-worker DB routing, deterministic module/serial grouping and safe connection budget. Make the guide's full command the normal backend command. Run focused area sets in each phase, then one full suite at slice closeout. | Two repeat full runs: identical collected/pass/skip totals to serial (account explicitly for new harness tests), zero DB skips, no flaky order dependency; record serial/template/2-worker/optional 3-worker wall times and peak sessions. If claiming concurrent full runs safe, run two simultaneous full invocations on distinct run-owned DB sets and prove both pass without `too many clients`; otherwise document one-at-a-time policy. |
+
+The independent reviewer inspects provisioning, cleanup, isolation, pass-count and timing artifacts, reruns focused high-risk files, and reruns full only when a finding needs it. A failure to establish deterministic parallel execution leaves serial-with-template as the documented full command; do not assert a parallel speedup. Timing target: substantial improvement against the measured phase-1 baseline; if none, report the measured cause and revise the mechanism before closeout.
+
+## Testing guide contract
+
+`backend/TESTING.md` shall say: one real-Postgres full backend suite at the end of each slice; per-phase focused/affected test files (include adjacent integration tests and host/gateway suites when interfaces cross); reviewers rerun full only for a finding that needs it, otherwise cite the implementer's same-revision full evidence and run relevant focused tests. At most one full suite on this machine at a time until two concurrent isolated full runs prove safe. Include PowerShell examples for S1 Foundation/bindings, S2 ingestion, S3 dispatch, S4 Finding/money and the complete suite, with explicit DB environment setup and zero-skips check. `tools/check-all.ps1` remains the separate required guard command.
+

PRE-S3-03 is likewise a queue/guard packet without a registry task node (`tasks/PRE-S3-03-delivery-checker-report.md`; `frontend/docs/architecture/v69-dependency-registry-pilot.json:55-60`), while PRE-S3-04 has a product projection node. Close only after independent review accepts unchanged coverage, deterministic result and measured speed on this Windows host.

+# T-test-speed — Backend test-suite turnaround
+
+Status: implementation-ready; activate immediately after reviewed/merged S4-02d, before ordinary next-work ranking, per `.agent/owner-process-speed-2026-10-14.md:6`. Tooling work, outside competitive W1 scoring. Size: M, split into three S phases. Branch: `task/T-test-speed-backend-suite` (from the then-current main). USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED: false. Independent review: required.
+
+## Outcome and limits
+
+Substantially reduce wall time for the complete, real-Postgres backend suite on this Windows development machine without dropping, skipping, weakening, or silently selecting tests. Record a same-revision, same-machine baseline and final timing, exact collected/passed/skipped/failed counts, database settings, worker count and command. A full run with the test DB unset is invalid proof. Preserve migration upgrade/downgrade, process, concurrency and advisory-lock coverage. No product UI or W1 panel claim.
+

+| `backend/tests/conftest.py` only chooses a writable pytest temp base; it does not provision or isolate Postgres. Preserve explicit `--basetemp` and its fallback behavior. | `backend/tests/conftest.py:1-4,73-91` |

+# T-test-speed — Backend test-suite turnaround
+
+Status: implementation-ready; activate immediately after reviewed/merged S4-02d, before ordinary next-work ranking, per `.agent/owner-process-speed-2026-10-14.md:6`. Tooling work, outside competitive W1 scoring. Size: M, split into three S phases. Branch: `task/T-test-speed-backend-suite` (from the then-current main). USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED: false. Independent review: required.
+
+## Outcome and limits
+
+Substantially reduce wall time for the complete, real-Postgres backend suite on this Windows development machine without dropping, skipping, weakening, or silently selecting tests. Record a same-revision, same-machine baseline and final timing, exact collected/passed/skipped/failed counts, database settings, worker count and command. A full run with the test DB unset is invalid proof. Preserve migration upgrade/downgrade, process, concurrency and advisory-lock coverage. No product UI or W1 panel claim.
+
+## Current code, checked 2026-10-14
+
+| Finding | Evidence |
+| --- | --- |
+| `ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL` is the real-Postgres switch; absent DB causes skips, and destructive fixtures require a `*_test` database. | `backend/tests/test_s1_foundation_postgres.py:21-24,66-81`; `backend/README-db.md:76-90` |
+| `backend/tests/conftest.py` only chooses a writable pytest temp base; it does not provision or isolate Postgres. Preserve explicit `--basetemp` and its fallback behavior. | `backend/tests/conftest.py:1-4,73-91` |
+| The module-scoped `engine` fixture drops/recreates `public`, drops `btree_gist`, runs Alembic to head, and disposes its engine. Function-scoped `store` truncates rows but returns a store with its own engine. | `backend/tests/test_s1_foundation_postgres.py:66-90`; `backend/tests/test_s1_bindings_postgres.py:64-82` |
+| S4 files import the S1 binding fixture rather than a common DB fixture; each importing module executes its own module fixture. A separate S3 demo test also resets and migrates. Migration-history tests deliberately downgrade/upgrade in place. | `backend/tests/test_s4_assumptions_postgres.py:27-29`; `backend/tests/test_s4_consequence_store_postgres.py:36-38,387-397`; `backend/tests/test_s3_demo_configuration.py:138-149`; `backend/tests/test_s4_evidence_consequence_work_postgres.py:464-544` |
+| Short-lived stores can retain connections until garbage collection; three S4 modules use per-test `gc.collect()` as a slot workaround. A drain test starts a child Python process against `URL`, and advisory locks are asserted. | `backend/tests/test_s4_consequence_store_postgres.py:26-33`; `backend/tests/test_s4_consequence_recompute_postgres.py:23-30`; `backend/tests/test_s4_power_demo_case_postgres.py:27-34`; `backend/tests/test_s4_evidence_drain_postgres.py:398-431` |
+| The normal `tools/check-all.ps1` command is nine repository guards, not pytest. Agent handoffs separately report full backend pytest plus guards, sometimes per phase. | `tools/check-all.ps1:8-13,70-80`; `.agent/S3-01-phase-1-handoff.md:35`; `.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-handoff.md:115` |
+| pytest currently has no xdist dependency or marker definitions. | `backend/pyproject.toml:23-39` |
+
+## Chosen approach and rejected shortcuts
+
+1. **First remove repeated head migrations from ordinary Postgres setup.** Build one clean, migrated `*_test` template for each run, then clone to isolated worker DBs with `CREATE DATABASE ... TEMPLATE` from an admin connection outside the template. Clones must use names ending `_test`; source template must have no open sessions, and all generated DBs need explicit owner/run identity and cleanup in `finally`. Assert migration head on each clone. Keep the existing destructive upgrade/downgrade tests on dedicated clones that no ordinary test shares. If cloning is unavailable under the local role, report it and keep the tested serial migration path; never fall back to a shared live DB while claiming isolation. Rework fixture ownership so ordinary module setup truncates and never drops another module's schema. Preserve any tests whose own reset/migration is their assertion.
+2. **Then use bounded pytest-xdist workers, one DB per worker.** Add xdist to backend dev dependencies. Use worker identity only for DB routing; child processes inherit the worker URL. Begin with `-n 2`, measure `-n 3` only if connection counts remain safe, and pick the fastest stable count. Do not use `--dist load` if order-sensitive modules share state; use module grouping or explicit serial group for migration/process/lock tests. One worker DB is not sufficient if tests in that worker race a child process or a module rebuilds schema: identify and serialize or isolate those cases before enabling the default command. No `-n auto` on this machine.
+3. **Bound connections rather than assuming worker DB isolation bounds sessions.** Give test-created SQLAlchemy engines small pools (initial candidate `pool_size=1, max_overflow=1`) and dispose stores/clients explicitly where ownership permits. Account for migration `NullPool`, child processes and in-test concurrency when sizing; collect actual peak `pg_stat_activity` and retain legitimate concurrency assertions. `gc.collect()` removal is optional only after proof that engine lifecycle is deterministic. Do not alter production pool defaults simply to improve tests.
+4. **Focused suites are a workflow speed gain, not a replacement full run.** Add documented area commands and, only if useful after collection audit, explicit `unit`/`postgres` markers for focused selection. Missing DB must remain visible as skipped and disqualify full proof; markers must never deselect tests from the full command. File-path focus can ship before marker changes. Avoid a broad auto-marker that mislabels mixed files such as `test_s3_demo_configuration.py:126-149`.
+
+## Implementation phases and proof
+
+| Phase | Build (one implementer context) | Focused proof / exit |
+| --- | --- | --- |
+| 1 (S) baseline and guide | Record serial full-suite baseline on a private `*_test` DB with `--durations=30`, collection count, skip reasons, peak sessions and wall time; inventory module migration calls, engine owners, child-process and lock tests. Add a short guide at `backend/TESTING.md` linked from `backend/README-db.md`; include exact area-focused commands. | Guide commands run; no test edits or changed pass/skip counts. Baseline has zero DB-related skips. Record environment and timing in review packet. |
+| 2 (S) template and lifecycle | Implement run-owned migrated template/clone harness; move common setup out of S1 test modules; preserve destructive migration tests on dedicated clone(s); explicit disposal and bounded test pools. Keep a serial full command working. | Focused S1/S2/S3/S4 Postgres files, migration downgrade/upgrade cases, child-process/advisory-lock cases and a serial full suite. Equal collected/pass/skip totals to phase-1 baseline on same revision (new harness tests count separately); zero DB skips; template cleanup after failure. Timing table serial before/after. |
+| 3 (S) bounded parallel | Add xdist command with proven per-worker DB routing, deterministic module/serial grouping and safe connection budget. Make the guide's full command the normal backend command. Run focused area sets in each phase, then one full suite at slice closeout. | Two repeat full runs: identical collected/pass/skip totals to serial (account explicitly for new harness tests), zero DB skips, no flaky order dependency; record serial/template/2-worker/optional 3-worker wall times and peak sessions. If claiming concurrent full runs safe, run two simultaneous full invocations on distinct run-owned DB sets and prove both pass without `too many clients`; otherwise document one-at-a-time policy. |
+
+The independent reviewer inspects provisioning, cleanup, isolation, pass-count and timing artifacts, reruns focused high-risk files, and reruns full only when a finding needs it. A failure to establish deterministic parallel execution leaves serial-with-template as the documented full command; do not assert a parallel speedup. Timing target: substantial improvement against the measured phase-1 baseline; if none, report the measured cause and revise the mechanism before closeout.
+
+## Testing guide contract
+
+`backend/TESTING.md` shall say: one real-Postgres full backend suite at the end of each slice; per-phase focused/affected test files (include adjacent integration tests and host/gateway suites when interfaces cross); reviewers rerun full only for a finding that needs it, otherwise cite the implementer's same-revision full evidence and run relevant focused tests. At most one full suite on this machine at a time until two concurrent isolated full runs prove safe. Include PowerShell examples for S1 Foundation/bindings, S2 ingestion, S3 dispatch, S4 Finding/money and the complete suite, with explicit DB environment setup and zero-skips check. `tools/check-all.ps1` remains the separate required guard command.
+
+## Registry and closeout
+
+No dependency-registry node: this is process/tooling work with no technical path to an approved W1 core panel. PRE-S3-03 is likewise a queue/guard packet without a registry task node (`tasks/PRE-S3-03-delivery-checker-report.md`; `frontend/docs/architecture/v69-dependency-registry-pilot.json:55-60`), while PRE-S3-04 has a product projection node. Queue row below records scheduling without inventing score or changing `queue_baseline_nodes`. Run `node frontend/docs/architecture/check_v69_dependency_registry.cjs` on planning and implementation diffs. Close only after independent review accepts unchanged coverage, deterministic result and measured speed on this Windows host.

+# T-test-speed — Backend test-suite turnaround
+
+Status: implementation-ready; activate immediately after reviewed/merged S4-02d, before ordinary next-work ranking, per `.agent/owner-process-speed-2026-10-14.md:6`. Tooling work, outside competitive W1 scoring. Size: M, split into three S phases. Branch: `task/T-test-speed-backend-suite` (from the then-current main). USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED: false. Independent review: required.
+
+## Outcome and limits
+
+Substantially reduce wall time for the complete, real-Postgres backend suite on this Windows development machine without dropping, skipping, weakening, or silently selecting tests. Record a same-revision, same-machine baseline and final timing, exact collected/passed/skipped/failed counts, database settings, worker count and command. A full run with the test DB unset is invalid proof. Preserve migration upgrade/downgrade, process, concurrency and advisory-lock coverage. No product UI or W1 panel claim.
+
+## Current code, checked 2026-10-14
+
+| Finding | Evidence |
+| --- | --- |
+| `ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL` is the real-Postgres switch; absent DB causes skips, and destructive fixtures require a `*_test` database. | `backend/tests/test_s1_foundation_postgres.py:21-24,66-81`; `backend/README-db.md:76-90` |
+| `backend/tests/conftest.py` only chooses a writable pytest temp base; it does not provision or isolate Postgres. Preserve explicit `--basetemp` and its fallback behavior. | `backend/tests/conftest.py:1-4,73-91` |
+| The module-scoped `engine` fixture drops/recreates `public`, drops `btree_gist`, runs Alembic to head, and disposes its engine. Function-scoped `store` truncates rows but returns a store with its own engine. | `backend/tests/test_s1_foundation_postgres.py:66-90`; `backend/tests/test_s1_bindings_postgres.py:64-82` |
+| S4 files import the S1 binding fixture rather than a common DB fixture; each importing module executes its own module fixture. A separate S3 demo test also resets and migrates. Migration-history tests deliberately downgrade/upgrade in place. | `backend/tests/test_s4_assumptions_postgres.py:27-29`; `backend/tests/test_s4_consequence_store_postgres.py:36-38,387-397`; `backend/tests/test_s3_demo_configuration.py:138-149`; `backend/tests/test_s4_evidence_consequence_work_postgres.py:464-544` |
+| Short-lived stores can retain connections until garbage collection; three S4 modules use per-test `gc.collect()` as a slot workaround. A drain test starts a child Python process against `URL`, and advisory locks are asserted. | `backend/tests/test_s4_consequence_store_postgres.py:26-33`; `backend/tests/test_s4_consequence_recompute_postgres.py:23-30`; `backend/tests/test_s4_power_demo_case_postgres.py:27-34`; `backend/tests/test_s4_evidence_drain_postgres.py:398-431` |
+| The normal `tools/check-all.ps1` command is nine repository guards, not pytest. Agent handoffs separately report full backend pytest plus guards, sometimes per phase. | `tools/check-all.ps1:8-13,70-80`; `.agent/S3-01-phase-1-handoff.md:35`; `.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-handoff.md:115` |
+| pytest currently has no xdist dependency or marker definitions. | `backend/pyproject.toml:23-39` |
+
+## Chosen approach and rejected shortcuts
+
+1. **First remove repeated head migrations from ordinary Postgres setup.** Build one clean, migrated `*_test` template for each run, then clone to isolated worker DBs with `CREATE DATABASE ... TEMPLATE` from an admin connection outside the template. Clones must use names ending `_test`; source template must have no open sessions, and all generated DBs need explicit owner/run identity and cleanup in `finally`. Assert migration head on each clone. Keep the existing destructive upgrade/downgrade tests on dedicated clones that no ordinary test shares. If cloning is unavailable under the local role, report it and keep the tested serial migration path; never fall back to a shared live DB while claiming isolation. Rework fixture ownership so ordinary module setup truncates and never drops another module's schema. Preserve any tests whose own reset/migration is their assertion.
+2. **Then use bounded pytest-xdist workers, one DB per worker.** Add xdist to backend dev dependencies. Use worker identity only for DB routing; child processes inherit the worker URL. Begin with `-n 2`, measure `-n 3` only if connection counts remain safe, and pick the fastest stable count. Do not use `--dist load` if order-sensitive modules share state; use module grouping or explicit serial group for migration/process/lock tests. One worker DB is not sufficient if tests in that worker race a child process or a module rebuilds schema: identify and serialize or isolate those cases before enabling the default command. No `-n auto` on this machine.
+3. **Bound connections rather than assuming worker DB isolation bounds sessions.** Give test-created SQLAlchemy engines small pools (initial candidate `pool_size=1, max_overflow=1`) and dispose stores/clients explicitly where ownership permits. Account for migration `NullPool`, child processes and in-test concurrency when sizing; collect actual peak `pg_stat_activity` and retain legitimate concurrency assertions. `gc.collect()` removal is optional only after proof that engine lifecycle is deterministic. Do not alter production pool defaults simply to improve tests.
+4. **Focused suites are a workflow speed gain, not a replacement full run.** Add documented area commands and, only if useful after collection audit, explicit `unit`/`postgres` markers for focused selection. Missing DB must remain visible as skipped and disqualify full proof; markers must never deselect tests from the full command. File-path focus can ship before marker changes. Avoid a broad auto-marker that mislabels mixed files such as `test_s3_demo_configuration.py:126-149`.
+
+## Implementation phases and proof
+
+| Phase | Build (one implementer context) | Focused proof / exit |
+| --- | --- | --- |
+| 1 (S) baseline and guide | Record serial full-suite baseline on a private `*_test` DB with `--durations=30`, collection count, skip reasons, peak sessions and wall time; inventory module migration calls, engine owners, child-process and lock tests. Add a short guide at `backend/TESTING.md` linked from `backend/README-db.md`; include exact area-focused commands. | Guide commands run; no test edits or changed pass/skip counts. Baseline has zero DB-related skips. Record environment and timing in review packet. |
+| 2 (S) template and lifecycle | Implement run-owned migrated template/clone harness; move common setup out of S1 test modules; preserve destructive migration tests on dedicated clone(s); explicit disposal and bounded test pools. Keep a serial full command working. | Focused S1/S2/S3/S4 Postgres files, migration downgrade/upgrade cases, child-process/advisory-lock cases and a serial full suite. Equal collected/pass/skip totals to phase-1 baseline on same revision (new harness tests count separately); zero DB skips; template cleanup after failure. Timing table serial before/after. |
+| 3 (S) bounded parallel | Add xdist command with proven per-worker DB routing, deterministic module/serial grouping and safe connection budget. Make the guide's full command the normal backend command. Run focused area sets in each phase, then one full suite at slice closeout. | Two repeat full runs: identical collected/pass/skip totals to serial (account explicitly for new harness tests), zero DB skips, no flaky order dependency; record serial/template/2-worker/optional 3-worker wall times and peak sessions. If claiming concurrent full runs safe, run two simultaneous full invocations on distinct run-owned DB sets and prove both pass without `too many clients`; otherwise document one-at-a-time policy. |
+
+The independent reviewer inspects provisioning, cleanup, isolation, pass-count and timing artifacts, reruns focused high-risk files, and reruns full only when a finding needs it. A failure to establish deterministic parallel execution leaves serial-with-template as the documented full command; do not assert a parallel speedup. Timing target: substantial improvement against the measured phase-1 baseline; if none, report the measured cause and revise the mechanism before closeout.
+
+## Testing guide contract
+
+`backend/TESTING.md` shall say: one real-Postgres full backend suite at the end of each slice; per-phase focused/affected test files (include adjacent integration tests and host/gateway suites when interfaces cross); reviewers rerun full only for a finding that needs it, otherwise cite the implementer's same-revision full evidence and run relevant focused tests. At most one full suite on this machine at a time until two concurrent isolated full runs prove safe. Include PowerShell examples for S1 Foundation/bindings, S2 ingestion, S3 dispatch, S4 Finding/money and the complete suite, with explicit DB environment setup and zero-skips check. `tools/check-all.ps1` remains the separate required guard command.
+
+## Registry and closeout
+
+No dependency-registry node: this is process/tooling work with no technical path to an approved W1 core panel. PRE-S3-03 is likewise a queue/guard packet without a registry task node (`tasks/PRE-S3-03-delivery-checker-report.md`; `frontend/docs/architecture/v69-dependency-registry-pilot.json:55-60`), while PRE-S3-04 has a product projection node. Queue row below records scheduling without inventing score or changing `queue_baseline_nodes`. Run `node frontend/docs/architecture/check_v69_dependency_registry.cjs` on planning and implementation diffs. Close only after independent review accepts unchanged coverage, deterministic result and measured speed on this Windows host.
